Abstract (英文): Camera-equipped AI glasses turn an examination page into a query for a remote vision-language model. Device detection targets possession, and our companion work SEOYUN targets the model. PEACE-TEST-PROTOCOL instead changes who owns the eyewear. Every candidate wears authority-issued glasses for the whole session, and the question paper is printed so that it can be read only through them. As in cinema stereoscopy, a patterned laminate encodes the text in the handedness of circular polarization rather than in brightness. The page looks uniformly grey to the naked eye and to unfiltered cameras, and the issued circular analyzer reveals the text. Compulsory issued eyewear turns the smart-glasses form factor against itself. The face position is occupied by a device the authority supplies and collects, and a camera anywhere else needs its own analyzer. Spectacle wearers receive prescription inserts in the issued frame, low-vision candidates may use an offline assistive mixed-reality tier, and blind candidates keep braille, audio, and screen-reader formats. We derive the luminance and contrast budget and show why circular keying tolerates head tilt. A simulation study supports the analysis. A spectral Mueller model confirms tilt invariance and invisibility to linear-polarization cameras, but a 1 degree axis misregistration leaks a few percent of contrast. A capture-and-OCR experiment on English and Korean text shows that video averaging can recover leakage too weak to read in a single frame, so leakage must be bounded against the adversary's total photon budget. Data from 151 observers and 28 cameras show that spectral (metameric) keys are visible to all of them. The key carries no secrecy, and filtered covert cameras remain possible. All results are simulations; no prototypes, physical measurements, or human-subject results are reported.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Visual creation demands expressive imagery together with precise control over text, layout, and structure. We introduce Nemotron-Labs-VisualCreation, an agentic framework that connects these capabilities through Pixel as Code. Raster images are represented by learned discrete visual tokens, while structured artifacts use explicit programs. A shared generation-and-rendering interface connects these representations to visual feedback across images, vector graphics, webpages, and documents. A Nemotron-native harness coordinates planning and raster candidate selection, with targeted repair for editable structured and hybrid artifacts. To support this interface, we develop Nemotron-Labs-Image-Tokenizer, the autoregressive raster generator Nemotron-Labs-Image-30B-A3B, and the structured-code model family Nemotron-Labs-VisionCode. On approximately 178K images spanning six visual domains, our tokenizer improves reconstruction PSNR from 26.08 to 29.57 dB over Emu3.5 IBQ. It also provides 7.13x faster encoding and 10.93x faster decoding at batch size one on A100. In standalone evaluation, one Nemotron-Labs-VisionCode variant surpasses Kimi K3.1 Pro on the aggregate across Chart2Code, MMSVG, Design2Code, and Image2Struct. At the system level, the Nemotron-native configuration raises GenEval2 Soft-TIFA GM from 31.71 to 52.56 using the same raster generator. With our best setting, Nemotron-Labs-VisualCreation achieves performance competitive with GPT Image 2 and Nano Banana Pro across most benchmarks with available comparisons.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A camouflaged object can blend into its surroundings at one scale and become distinguishable at another. Yet a region's apparent ambiguity gives limited guidance on which observation would resolve it. The challenge is to connect the form and scale of concealment to the evidence that a new view can reveal. We introduce CamoBreak, an active framework for camouflaged object detection guided by a Concealment Spectrum. From the image and current predictions, the spectrum measures regional resemblance and boundary ambiguity across comparison scales, distinguishing persistent concealment from separation emerging at finer or broader scales. These measurements guide crop selection from the already-captured image and recognition against global foreground and background references. An object-conditioned spatial preference then selects where local evidence should update the prediction and feature memory, retaining target corrections while suppressing background distractions. Accepted corrections reshape the next measurement, while predicted segmentation benefit determines whether to observe again. Experiments across encoder families and related segmentation tasks show consistent improvements. Targeted analyses support improved small-target recovery, multiple-object coverage, and selective evidence integration. Code will be released.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We introduce CoRe, in which understanding and generation jointly shape one contextual representation for images and one for text, trained from scratch. Each representation is read for understanding, conditions the other modality, and is the space in which that modality is generated by a continuous flow from Gaussian noise. With 383M parameters trained on CC12M and no pretrained language model, text encoder, or VAE at inference, CoRe matches a same-data contrastive-only baseline in global understanding and reaches COCO-30K FID 10.10, comparable to much larger generators built on pretrained components. In controlled comparisons at equal data and architecture, CoRe generates better in both directions (text to image and image to text) than a representation shaped by understanding alone and then frozen, and follows prompts better than generating in a space that understanding does not shape. In the other direction, with an attentive segmentation probe, on monocular depth, and when read by a frozen language model, CoRe surpasses the contrastive-only baseline: generation adds information that stronger readouts reach. Because generation runs inside the understanding representation, frozen real-image probes read and steer intermediate states and the model ranks its own samples without extra training. Because both modalities use continuous flows, both also distill to a few steps. Our controls indicate why one representation suffices: understanding organizes the representation semantically, while generation makes it retain more information.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Gaussian splatting from sparse training views renders high-quality images near training views, but reconstruction quality degrades at distant regions, which are undersampled by the training set. Feed-forward novel view synthesis (NVS) models use strong learned priors to resample the scene from substantially different viewpoints, but they take more than ten times as long to render and produce high-frequency artifacts on out-of-domain scenes. We propose a plug-and-play method that enhances an existing sparse-view Gaussian model with a pretrained feed-forward NVS model. We observe that the two are complementary in the frequency domain: the prior is more accurate at low frequencies, and the Gaussians at high frequencies. The reliability of the prior relative to the Gaussians is inversely proportional to the image frequency, so the optimal fusion weight is a first-order low-pass filter with one parameter that can be fitted. Our key idea is then to intelligently fuse the reliable part of each of the two methods. To remove the slow feed-forward model at render time, we use these fused images as teachers and distill them back into Gaussians, balanced with supervision from input views. Our method improves the quality of various sparse-view Gaussian methods without sacrificing rendering speed, and our analysis works on different prior models. Project page: https://linwk20.github.io/spectral-fusion-gs/

</details>

Abstract (英文): Characterizing how tornadoes evolve over space and time is critically important for understanding and preparing for these highly destructive weather events. Towards this end, we develop algorithms capable of forming time-resolved 3D reconstructions of laboratory smoke tornadoes from sparse one-sided videos. Even in a controlled environment, reconstruction is severely underconstrained: appearance is view dependent, motion is non-rigid, and the unobserved volume admits many geometries. We present Tornado4D, a physics-guided 4D Gaussian framework capable of reconstructing laboratory tornadoes from sparse, one-sided videos. Tornado4D uses knowledge of the enclosure geometry to introduce rotational airflow field priors that constrain how the 3D structure evolves over time. These rotational priors allow us to reconstruct otherwise unobserved structure. We evaluate reconstruction accuracy by computing novel-view synthesis accuracy on a held out camera view: Tornado4D outperforms frame-by-frame 3DGS and video-based 4DGS by a considerable margin.

</details>

Abstract (英文): When is an image ellipse the outline of a given ellipsoid with semi-axes $a <= b <= c? For a camera with square pixels, zero skew and known principal point, we show that it is an outline at focal length $f$ exactly when a cone ratio kappa(f), computed from two eigenvalues, is at most a/c; the middle semi-axis b plays no role. With f unknown, the admissible focal lengths form an interval; whether it is empty is decided by the smallest cone ratio over $f$, which, when attained, is obtained from an explicit quartic. The interval is bounded above only when the principal point is closer to the long axis of the ellipse than to its short axis. Thus one ellipse can rule out ellipsoids that are too round, never ones that are too elongated, and none if the principal point is unknown. Simulations and 45 Cassini images of Saturn's moons support the test.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision-Language-Action (VLA) models effectively learn action policies from robot demonstrations, but struggle to generalize to changes in object positions and task goals. General multimodal foundation models (MFMs) offer complementary scene understanding and spatial reasoning, but remain slow and unreliable for direct fine-grained control. To understand how MFMs can improve VLA spatial generalization, we conduct a systematic capability analysis across multiple VLA architectures using atomic-stage interventions, revealing that many failures stem from poor target grounding and global spatial routing, while local manipulation skills remain largely usable. Based on these findings, we propose MFM-VLA, a selective collaboration framework in which an MFM provides target grounding and spatial guidance, controllers execute free-space motion, and the VLA retains control over fine-grained manipulation. We fine-tune the VLA with action-chunk-level dense geometric captions and perturbed-state recovery data to improve local guidance following and recovery from unfamiliar handoff states. A development-calibrated capability router selects between direct VLA execution and MFM-assisted execution to avoid unnecessary intervention. Across LIBERO-PRO, ALOHA-Sim-PRO, RoboCasa365, and real-world chemistry tasks, MFM-VLA improves success rates by 14.9, 74.0, 3.6, and 30.9 percentage points, respectively, over the corresponding base VLA. The project page is available at https://tom-zgt.github.io/MFM-VLA

</details>

Abstract (英文): Retrieving images of patients clinically similar to a given patient can support diagnosis, teaching and research. Training and evaluating retrieval systems requires knowing which images of different patients show the same finding, and existing datasets are small or narrow in scope. We construct PMC-PaIR, a dataset of 86,144 pairs of clinically similar images from 56,013 open-access PubMed Central case reports, which often document rare or challenging presentations. We develop an approach that follows citations, which often link case reports of similar patients. To determine what two linked cases share clinically, it prompts large language models to generate and answer hierarchical questions, from modality to diagnosis and findings, and to match images by caption using the answers. Experts find the pairs highly accurate, and this question-guided approach finds twice as many correct pairs as direct matching. Fine-tuning BiomedCLIP on PMC-PaIR improves retrieval in all modality, anatomy and diagnosis categories tested, for image-only queries and image queries with a short text naming the finding. We release the dataset and the automated pipeline, which can extend PMC-PaIR as new case reports are published.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent advances in video generation have made automated advertising production increasingly feasible. However, an effective advertisement must faithfully represent the product and communicate its selling points through compelling visual storytelling, which requires more than visual synthesis: it calls for deeper product understanding and strategic decisions about how to present product value. Crucially, product research and creative design are closely intertwined, as the information gathered about a product determines which features can be faithfully demonstrated and how they should be presented. In this paper, we introduce AdProducer, the first end-to-end agentic framework for advertising video creation, transforming concise creative briefs into finished advertisements. Our multimodal agent autonomously researches products through web and image search and translates its findings into selling-point-driven storyboards that guide a frozen video generator. To achieve this, we develop a dedicated data construction pipeline that yields two training datasets, AdProducer-SFT and AdProducer-RL, and train the agent through supervised fine-tuning followed by reinforcement learning with joint optimization of storyboard- and video-level rewards. We further introduce AdCreation-Bench, comprising 305 advertising briefs across eight domains, with two complementary metrics: PG-Score for product correctness and creative execution, and Ad-Score for overall advertising effectiveness assessed by an independent judge. Experiments demonstrate that AdProducer improves the PG-Score of MiniMax H3 from 17.3 to 51.3 and generalizes to Veo 3.1 without additional training, improving its score from 28.0 to 35.9, with consistent gains in Ad-Score, VideoScore2, and human evaluations. Code, models, and data are released.

</details>

Abstract (英文): This paper is concerned with prompt-image alignment at inference time through exploration of generative trajectories and dynamical prompt rejuvenation. We leverage sequential Monte Carlo (SMC), to offer a gradient-free and principled framework for this purpose through reward-guided trajectory selection. More specifically, we introduce LLM-based prompt rejuvenation, allowing for efficient particle exploration in SMC samplers along the diffusion time in flow matching models. The proposed method, PromptSMC, retains the selected latent states and uses a pretrained LLM to dynamically revise the prompts of duplicated particles under semantic-preservation constraints. These revised prompts induce alternative conditional velocity fields, allowing particles to explore different continuations from a shared intermediate state. The method further uses a reward model to evaluate each particle's alignment with the original prompt, and the resulting scores guide particles through resampling. PromptSMC thus integrates prompt-based exploration with reward-guided sampling, achieving faithful prompt-image alignment without additional noise injection, model training, or reward gradients. Our experiments and ablation studies across multiple benchmarks and backbones demonstrate the effectiveness of the method for text-to-image flow matching. Code is available at https://github.com/shermanlian/PromptSMC.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Multi-view 3D foundation models estimate camera poses and 3D points from a set of photos in one forward pass. Many cameras store a portrait shot sideways, with an orientation tag telling viewers how to turn it, and this tag can be ignored, lost or wrong. We measure how rotated photos affect five such models on three datasets. A few rotated photos lower camera pose accuracy for every model, and the sideways photos already in a standard benchmark do the same. Even the most robust model still loses accuracy. The failure is also silent: the models' confidence does not reliably flag it, and most official image loaders ignore the tag. The fixes we test each correct only part of the problem. These findings call for orientation checks in reconstruction pipelines and benchmarks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Verifying image authenticity is a critical need for a variety of applications and decision-making in the remote sensing (RS) domain, yet it is becoming more challenging as advancements in generative AI enable producing increasingly realistic synthetic overhead data. However, existing RS deepfake benchmarks prioritize sheer volume at the expense of visual realism and diversity, while also lacking rigorous out-of-distribution (OOD) testing protocols to evaluate real-world robustness. To address this, we introduce the Geospatial Deepfakes Dataset (GDD), a benchmark for RS deepfake detection and manipulation localization. Rather than relying on uncurated, redundant synthetic generation, GDD comprises images across diverse modern generative models and real image sources, alongside manipulations from diffusion inpainting, splicing, and copy-move operations. Crucially, GDD establishes an OOD evaluation protocol for deepfake detection comprising unseen real image sources and generative models. We benchmark five deepfake detectors and two localization models across intra- and cross-dataset regimes and reveal that detectors trained on high-volume, uncurated benchmarks overfit to dataset-specific artifacts, whereas training on GDD yields superior cross-dataset generalization despite its compact scale. This benchmarking also exposes critical failure modes in current state-of-the-art methods. Combined, GDD and its evaluation protocol establish a high-quality standard for developing and stress-testing detection and localization models in this safety-critical RS domain. The dataset is publicly available.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral image (HSI) restoration has been explored using deep learning techniques recently. Because hyperspectral images have strong spectral and spatial redundancy, the underlying clean signal is usually represented in a low-dimensional spectral subspace and locally low-rank spatial structures. Existing methods primarily explore dense attention or feature mixing across spectral bands. In this work, we propose HyMTS, a hyperspectral restoration network with a backbone built on the Multi-scale Tensorial Summation (MTS) factorization operator. HyMTS provides a natural architectural mechanism for exploiting a low-rank prior, embedded directly in the network operator rather than imposed via an auxiliary loss or a constraint on the reconstructed output. Specifically, HyMTS stacks a unified block that couples factorized layers with input-adaptive layers, capturing both local and global dependencies across the spectral and spatial dimensions. Extensive experiments without auxiliary data on standard benchmarks for Gaussian and real-noise denoising and single-image super-resolution show that HyMTS efficiently achieves superior or comparable performance to various state-of-the-art approaches.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Robotic perception for capturing uncooperative orbital objects and inspecting variable industrial products must often operate with limited labelled imagery. We examine a modular synthetic-augmentation pipeline, evaluating generation planning and post-generation filtering as separate interventions, with computational cost as a design constraint. Across six object-detection datasets, FLUX.1-Fill with class-specific LoRA adaptation produced dataset-dependent dose effects; the validation-selected augmentation improved mAP@50:95 on four datasets and reduced it on two. Filtering did not yield consistent gains, and FID, KID, precision, recall, and LPIPS did not consistently track detector changes. The results do not establish that image quality alone identifies useful samples. They motivate standardised evaluation and inexpensive candidate-utility measures that could identify useful samples before detector retraining, reducing repeated training cost.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Initialization-free bundle adjustment (InitFree BA) aims to jointly recover camera poses and scene structure without requiring an initial global estimate. It is a very challenging problem, especially compared to traditional BA where a good estimation is assumed. While recent methods optimize surrogate Object Space Error (OSE) objectives from random initialization, a low objective value does not necessarily yield an accurate reconstruction, revealing a gap between optimization and reconstruction. We show that existing InitFree BA pipelines struggle to produce accurate 3D reconstructions on modern SfM benchmarks, and demonstrate that incorporating learned metric depth substantially improves their practical feasibility. We introduce InitFree-SfM, a complete initialization-free SfM pipeline combining learned correspondences, metric depth, depth-consistent track construction, and joint metric refinement. Within this framework, we propose a simple depth-augmented OSE (dOSE), which complements the directional constraints of OSE by constraining landmark positions along their viewing rays. Our approach substantially outperforms existing InitFree BA methods and achieves competitive accuracy with established SfM pipelines, including on challenging low-overlap image collections. Importantly, our analysis challenges the prevailing emphasis on surrogate objectives in previous InitFree BA research, revealing that geometric constraints play a central role in accurate reconstruction, while OSE-based optimization provides further improvements.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We introduce an image compression framework with three properties: (i) adaptation of pretrained codecs without retraining or fine-tuning, (ii) cycle-consistent recompression, even of already-encoded images, and (iii) decoding with independently chosen generative priors. We wrap the frozen encoder with non-learned operations so that every continuous latent has a preimage, yielding a surjective pseudo-invertible neural network. A nonlinear right inverse built from the pretrained decoder satisfies $E'(D'(E'(x)))=E'(x)$. To extend consistency to quantized bitstreams, the receiver searches for a continuous latent that reproduces both the transmitted main symbols and entropy side information. A successful search and an exact correction preserve both streams under deterministic coding; the final reconstruction is checked through the actual coding path. The encoder's pseudo-inverse enables a nonlinear counterpart of back-projection. In the linear case, back-projection has the closed-form solution $x'=x+A^\dagger(y-Ax)$, giving the closest reconstruction to $x$ satisfying $Ax'=y$. Our nonlinear formulation enforces the transmitted code on proposals from an independently chosen generative model. Adjusting the starting noise controls the prior's influence without changing that code. Experiments show small first-pass quality changes, higher retained quality than trained consistency codecs over 50 recompressions, also under 8-bit storage, and decoder-side control of the perception-distortion trade-off. Decoupling the codec from its prior lets the receiver adopt future generators without retraining or re-encoding.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Data augmentation is essential for training deep learning models that are generalizable, particularly when training data are limited. In brain MRI, augmentation methods that synthesize anatomically diverse images without relying on large cohorts are particularly attractive, especially when training supervised models for scans with a specific resolution or MRI contrast. While conventional random nonlinear fields offer a simple means of achieving such diversity, they fail to capture the underlying spatial dependencies among morphological changes across brain regions. To address this limitation, we introduce BrainAug, an anatomy-aware augmentation framework that generates diverse, anatomically coordinated deformations without relying on population imaging data. BrainAug constructs an anatomical graph encoding spatial proximity and bilateral correspondence between brain regions, then incorporates these relationships into a probabilistic model designed to sample correlated regional volume changes. These sampled variations are translated into diffeomorphic transformations, enabling substantial structural changes while preserving anatomical topology. Combined with contrast-agnostic image synthesis, BrainAug generates training images with independently varying anatomy and appearance. Evaluations across eight brain MRI datasets demonstrate stronger segmentation generalization across diverse populations, while registration experiments show improved alignment under substantial morphological differences.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Cognitive neuroscience learned that an activation map is not a map of necessity. Regions that light up during a task are not always needed for it, and only lesions reveal which ones are. Mixture-of-experts (MoE) vision-language models come with an activation map for free, since the router records which experts respond to image and text tokens, and recent work reads modality-specialized experts off this map. We bring the lesion. In Qwen3-VL-30B-A3B, Kimi-VL-A3B and Gemma-4-26B-A4B, 3 to 12% of the experts are vision-exclusive: image tokens route to them at up to five times the uniform rate and text tokens almost never. In Qwen3-VL they receive a quarter of all image-token routing, and lesioning all of them costs less than one percentage point on average over eight perception benchmarks and free-form OCR, no more than lesioning a random set of the same size. In Kimi-VL and Gemma-4 the lesion also costs no more than a random set. Scoped lesions, which remove an expert set only at image positions or only at text positions, then show where the models do depend on their experts. In Qwen3-VL, silencing every expert at every image position costs 3 points, against 45 at text positions, where the dependence sits in dual-use experts that text-only tasks need as well. Across models the image-side dependence is 2 to 3 points in Qwen3-VL and Kimi-VL and 26 in Gemma-4. Reading text in images is the one ability that needs image-position experts in every model. The exclusive experts receive informative patches, produce outputs of ordinary size, and stay inert under sensory deprivation: text-only training recruits them only when load balancing forces it. In Kimi-VL the balancing bias itself manufactures most of them. Load balancing draws the modality map in the part of the computation the models depend on least. Expert selection, pruning and skipping should follow the lesion, not the map.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vision Transformers (ViTs) have achieved strong performance across computer vision tasks, but the quality of their learned representations differs drastically depending on the training paradigm. While self-supervised ViTs often demonstrate emergent properties, such as object-aligned patch representations and attention maps, supervised ViTs typically lack these properties, even when trained with strong data augmentations. Understanding the origin of this difference is important for explaining and controlling the object-level structure. To investigate it, we perform a spectral analysis of supervised and self-supervised models, and identify a key distinction: MLP layers in supervised ViTs introduce high-frequency variations into patch representations, disrupting their spatial organization. Motivated by this finding, we propose Spec$^2$R, a lightweight spectral regularization method that fine-tunes the MLP layers of pretrained supervised ViTs. Spec$^2$R recovers object-aligned patch representations and attention maps, while improving adversarial robustness and preserving competitive classification accuracy.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Real editing requests describe a look, not the steps to reach it, so an agent that serves them must judge how much of each tool the look takes. Existing benchmarks do not measure this: their tasks either state the tools and values, or leave the amount open and are scored by VLM judges that pass more than half of half- and triple-strength results. We introduce GoldiBench, a benchmark of 500 editing tasks in five professional applications (darktable, RawTherapee, GIMP, Inkscape, Blender), solvable by writing a program or by operating the GUI. Each task pairs a START image with a TARGET rendered from a hidden program, so the amount is fixed by a known target; the agent's edit is re-rendered and compared with TARGET, and the same task is posed with the tools and values, the tools only, or the goal only, separating operation from effect judgement. Across 25 open, GUI and closed models, agents operate well (99-100% with programs from 4B up when told the values) but fall off a cliff when they must judge the amount: given only the tools, the best model passes 19% of the tasks with programs and 33% through the GUI, and scale does not close the gap. Manuals barely help; the measured TARGET-START difference more than doubles the pass rate, but the same difference shown as an image does not for open models. Allowed four renders, agents almost always stop after the first; forced to compare, the strong models double their pass rate. Agents know the tools, but neither read nor check the amount.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Learning spatial representations often relies on explicitly predicting geometric quantities, but how physical geometry is organized within learned representations remains less understood. We ask whether 3D structure can emerge from learning relations across 2D observations, without 3D prediction targets. We introduce RePT, which learns compact relations between distinct physical points through cross-view 2D point prediction. The learned relation space develops a geometric organization in which magnitudes, angles, and neighborhoods reflect physical 3D displacement. A single linear readout fitted on 16 scenes recovers displacement length and angle on 39 held-out scenes without refitting (Pearson r = 0.72 and 0.78). The ARKitScenes-trained checkpoint also retains strong geometric organization on ScanNet++, showcasing generalizability. In controlled comparisons, distinct-point relational prediction consistently yields stronger geometric organization than matched same-point correspondence learning. This organization depends on additive relations and adaptive scene context, and higher point-transfer accuracy does not by itself produce stronger organization. More broadly, our results suggest that 3D geometry need not be an explicit prediction target and can instead emerge as a structural property of representations trained to relate 2D observations, pointing to relational prediction as a complementary route to learning the representations that spatial intelligence builds on.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Object detectors often transfer poorly to specialized domains, where box annotations are scarce and deployment requires compact, real-time models. Large vision foundation models can provide offline supervision, but their zero-shot predictions may be poorly aligned with the target domain. We present an adapt-label-distill pipeline that uses limited target annotations to specialize a foundation teacher and transfer its supervision to compact detectors. We adapt SAM3 on the labeled subset, generate pseudo-labels for the remaining unlabeled images, and train YOLOv11 students with confidence-guided supervision. Teacher confidence weights pseudo-label losses, while zero-gating retains uncertain hypotheses during assignment without applying their associated training losses. Experiments across six target domains and three student capacities show substantial gains in several domains, while revealing limited or negative transfer in others. Teacher comparisons under a common hard-label training recipe support the choice of SAM3, and comparisons with an EfficientTeacher adaptation provide additional evidence against an online SSOD baseline. With YOLOv11m, our pipeline improves over STAC-style self-training by up to 15.3 AP and reaches 51.6 AP on Garbage, compared with 54.1 under full supervision. On NVIDIA Jetson AGX Orin, the deployed student achieves 188 FPS versus 2.9 FPS for SAM3, benefiting from foundation-model supervision without incurring the teacher's deployment cost. Code is available at https://github.com/zk2172-columbia/ProjectAdaptLabelDistill_ObjectDetection.

Abstract (英文): Concept-based models justify a decision by concepts they claim to see, decoded through an explicit ontology. Yet concept predictions are known to leak label information, ignore locality, and be poorly grounded on new attribute combinations. A decision decoded from such concepts can satisfy the ontology and still be wrong, so we ask when it can serve as a certificate. On unseen combinations of familiar parts, a joint concept bottleneck model and a concept embedding model, trained as is standard, certify wrong classes 93-94% of the time despite a correct ontology (60% for a joint model trained only on visible parts and gated by a localizer). The joint model's part errors copy co-occurring values more often than chance (cross-concept shortcutting). Treating the ontology as an error-detecting code, we show that a certificate needs visible evidence excluding every rival, unseen parts reported as such, and rare misreads not driven by a shared shortcut. A minimal reference design, OCVE, reads each part from a local crop in its own forward pass. On unseen FunnyBirds classes, it certifies 52% of images at 0.4% risk, against 14% for mask-pooled features and 33% for full-image part readers (5.6% when trained and gated alike), at similar zero-shot accuracy. The best baselines, even calibrated or restricted to unseen classes, and a decoder counting only agreeing parts remain about 3-8 times riskier. On CUB, isolated region readers are more accurate than an attribute-prototype model (63.1% vs. 55.3%) and certify unseen species at about 6 points less risk than it and about 10 points less than features pooled in the same keypoint boxes. On real traffic signs, isolated readers lower certified risk from 99.8% to 67%, which is still far too high; most remaining errors are misread pictograms. On seen classes the ontology brings no gain over a fairly trained black box, and isolation costs accuracy.

</details>

Abstract (英文): We propose the multi-scale sliced Wasserstein (MSW) distance between image distributions, which operates in pixel space and requires no trained network. MSW convolves images with a fixed bank of discrete cosine filters at several scales, pools the responses over all positions, and compares the resulting one-dimensional distributions using the Wasserstein distance. We pair MSW with an exact two-sample test that gives calibrated p-values. Most metrics compare two image sets in the latent space of a pretrained network. We show that these metrics are insensitive to simple perturbations of the images, because the latent space is nearly invariant to them. In contrast, MSW detects smaller perturbations than latent-space metrics and needs far fewer images to do so. Although it uses no learned features, it still detects semantic changes of the image distribution. Finally, MSW matches or outperforms existing Wasserstein-based metrics in our experiments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Pretrained latent diffusion models are effective priors for transfer learning, from geometry estimation to physics simulation. Existing approaches fine-tune the diffusion backbone but freeze the encoder of the VAE to preserve the latent distribution. However, a frozen encoder may miss salient details in non-RGB inputs and is limited to three channels. We introduce Diffusion Prior Anchoring (DPA), a regularizer applied during VAE fine-tuning that uses a score-distillation update to steer latents toward the high-density manifold on which the pretrained diffusion model denoises accurately. We validate DPA across three image and video models (FLUX 3, LTX-Video, Wan2.2) and four tasks: monocular depth estimation, MRI reconstruction, wind-flow simulation, and surface normal estimation, where DPA lowers the prediction error by 7.5% relative to the frozen VAE, 4.9% to decoder-only fine-tuning, and 4.0% to unanchored VAE fine-tuning. Its gain grows the further the target data lies from the RGB training distribution, from 3.3% on monocular depth estimation to 7.2% on complex-valued MRI. DPA also applies to modified VAEs, including pruned and multi-channel variants. With a four-channel VAE that encodes depth and surface normals, it lowers depth estimation error by 9.3%.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Coordinate networks with ReLU activations perform poorly on implicit neural representation tasks, and the activations that address this, such as sinusoids and Gabor wavelets, all leave the piecewise-linear function class, so it remains unclear which property of ReLU is at fault. We introduce JaSIN, a family of bounded piecewise-linear activations that interpolates between ReLU and a piecewise-linear sinusoid without any the use of transcendental operation. Our results show that a bounded range improves image reconstruction PSNR on average by 0.06 dB, a single bump 3.29 dB, and centering that bump a further 3.64 dB. The oscillation count improves 6.30 dB more, raising ReLU from 24.0 to 37.3 dB on Kodak images, placing third over eleven activations, within 1.3 dB of the state-of-the-art, though beyond four oscillations the reconstruction collapses. Because an INR is fit on the grid on which it is evaluated, a unit whose pre-activations never reach the support of its activation is certifiably inactive over the entire domain; deleting such units is lossless without fine-tuning, removing up to 84% of parameters at zero loss in performance (dB) and decoding 3.2 times faster. Reference code and demos are available at https://github.com/jeovafarias/jasin.

</details>

Abstract (英文): For decades, every new medical image segmentation task has needed an expert, a dedicated architecture, and a framework such as nnU-Net. This is no longer the case: medical image segmentation is now just another coding task. Given only a task description, labeled data, and a CPU (with no segmentation framework, no pretrained weights, and no human in the loop), four off-the-shelf coding agents built segmenters for 13 tasks across four imaging modalities within hours, an ability that has emerged rather than been built. On the nine 2D tasks, their best programs reach 99% of the Dice of a GPU-trained nnU-Net. Like any code, a segmenter improves by being revised and checked, so we ask how far the same agents can improve their own segmentations and what unlocks further gains. On unseen cases there is no reference, so the agents inspect renderings of their own predictions: this alone lifts the worst tenth of cases by 0.09 Dice and cuts boundary error (HD95) by almost a quarter. Far more is within reach. Told the true Dice of each attempt, the same agents improve in all 14 matched pairs, rescue 85% of failed masks instead of 30%, and surpass the GPU-trained nnU-Net on average, by producing better masks rather than by choosing among them. What unlocks these gains is a quality signal, which neither visual inspection, nor more rounds, nor a quality estimator trained by the agents themselves yet supplies: we call this the quality blind spot. The ability to build and to repair is already present; the next advance will come not from another segmentation model, but from reference-free quality estimation that coding agents can call.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Robotic manipulation requires predicting how robot-object interactions evolve over time, encompassing both visual appearance and scene geometry. In multi-camera settings, such predictions need to capture the evolving 3D scene consistently across viewpoints, while supporting controllable generation under different robot actions and camera configurations. Existing video generation models provide strong priors for visual appearance and motion but typically lack explicit geometric modeling. Meanwhile, recent advances in geometry-aware and multi-view generation have explored joint appearance-geometry modeling and cross-view consistency, yet generating temporally coherent appearance and geometry across multiple viewpoints under robotic manipulation conditions remains challenging. In this work, we propose GeoMVGen, a framework for joint multi-view RGB and pointmap video generation in robotic manipulation. Built on a video diffusion transformer, our framework employs modality-specific adaptation modules to capture the distinct characteristics of appearance and geometry, while using cross-modal residual connections to facilitate information exchange between the two modalities. To establish geometric correspondences across viewpoints, we further introduce a cross-view projection mechanism with complementary geometric supervisions, combining pointmap consistency with geometry foundation model-guided feature alignment. These designs enable controllable generation of temporally evolving multi-view observations with coherent appearance and geometrically consistent scene structures. Extensive experiments on 4D video generation dataset comprising simulated and real-world robotic manipulation scenarios demonstrate that GeoMVGen outperforms state-of-the-art methods in video quality, geometric accuracy, and cross-view consistency.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Image restoration remains a fundamental challenge in low-level vision, requiring methods that handle diverse degradations while preserving scene structures and details. We revisit classical Retinex theory and propose a Generalized Retinex formulation that extends the illumination-reflectance decomposition into a Retinex-inspired content-environment factorization prior for image restoration. The key idea is to restore images using two learned factors: a content-dominant response (generalized reflectance) capturing scene properties and an environment-side response (generalized illumination) encoding task-dependent variations associated with the observed degradations. Based on this formulation, we develop GRUN (Generalized Retinex Unfolding Network), a MAP-guided deep unfolding framework that alternates reflectance restoration, illumination estimation, and progressive degradation inference across K stages with degradation-conditioned proximal operators. We further introduce a Degradation-aware Coupling Regularizer (DCR) that combines mutual information minimization, gradient decorrelation, and scale anchoring to promote complementary content-environment factor representations. Experiments on seven image restoration tasks, including denoising, dehazing, deraining, low-light enhancement, deblurring, underwater enhancement, and backlit enhancement, demonstrate competitive results on multiple benchmarks using the same architecture with task-specific weights. Moreover, the proposed Generalized Retinex design can be integrated into existing restoration methods, demonstrating its cross-architecture applicability. Code will be released.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Retired data-center hardware can often be refurbished or resold, but triage demands more than object categories. A model must tell visually similar parts apart, judge condition under damage and occlusion, and read the printed part number. We audit nine public e-waste collections. They cover mostly consumer devices (only 10% of images are labeled as a data-center part), label neither condition nor printed text, and contain 42% exact or augmented duplicates. We introduce E-Waste-Bench, 328K photographs drawn from 762K deduplicated images and labeled for class (24 classes), granularity, condition and printed text. Images come from those collections and the web, where a recipe-based crawler works beside an agent built on an open-weight vision-language model. The agent vets new sources and reads sites the crawler cannot parse, and both are steered toward under-represented classes. Training labels are model-made, splits follow near-duplicate groups, and human annotators label a test set of nearly 10,000 images. We benchmark classification, condition, detection, text perception and cross-dataset transfer. On this test set, a ConvNeXt-T trained on E-Waste-Bench reaches 64.3% top-1, against 31.5% with the public collections alone. Near-duplicate leakage inflates top-1 by 3.0 points under model-made test labels but by less than 0.1 under human labels, so model-labeled benchmarks need group splits. Accuracy falls to 42.1% on marketplace and auction photographs, the closest proxy for hardware leaving service. Data and code will be released.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Describing an image requires not only recognizing what is visible but deciding what should be inferred. We investigate a systematic gap between humans and VLMs in their tendency to attribute visible actions or events to people who are not present in an image. We term this behavior unwarranted person attribution bias and find that, across 25 SOTA VLMs, captions of person-absent scenes mention a person four times as often as human-written captions of the same images. Through a preference study, we further show that both humans and VLMs prefer captions that avoid such attributions. Motivated by this, we introduce NOBODY, a preference dataset designed to bridge the gap between humans and VLMs in unwarranted person attribution. Through direct preference optimization (DPO), we align open VLMs on NOBODY and show that their agreement with human captions on person attribution reaches or exceeds inter-human agreement, whereas nine existing public preference datasets leave this behavior largely unchanged. Furthermore, we show that human judges preferred the aligned VLM captions significantly more than those of the baseline model. Alongside NOBODY, we release NOBODY-Bench, which evaluates VLMs for unwarranted person attribution bias and their alignment against human captions and preference. Project page: https://pmadinei.github.io/NOBODY/

</details>

Abstract (英文): Event cameras report asynchronous brightness changes, yet metric-depth supervision is typically available only at sparsely sampled reference timestamps. Aggregating events into frame-like representations simplifies learning but can obscure the fine-grained temporal structure of the original event stream. We introduce EventSSM, a causal spatial-state framework for event-associated metric-depth estimation using a recurrent memory updated by chronological event packets. To bridge asynchronous predictions and frame-time supervision, predicted event depths are geometrically transported using calibrated camera poses and differentiably rendered at reference timestamps. Benchmark evaluations demonstrate improvements over established event-depth methods across multiple depth-error measures. Controlled ablations further examine cross-modal initialization, temporal recurrence, individual state, readout, and supervision components, and packet subdivision. Together, these findings establish EventSSM as an effective framework for causal event-associated metric-depth estimation, combining persistent spatial context with physically timed state retention.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Understanding a group conversation means deciding, from video and audio, who is speaking and to whom. Leading systems treat these tasks offline on single-channel clips and align models with a self-evaluation reward. We propose BEARING, a geometry-first alternative built on one measured signal: the direction from which a microphone array beside the camera hears a sound. A label-free calibration learned from the stream registers this direction into the camera image, and an online registry of faces, voices and directions needs no enrollment. The registered direction serves as (i) a visual prompt for a vision-language model, a marker on the speaker or an arrow toward an off-screen source; (ii) one input of a reliability-weighted fusion with visual active-speaker detection, for speakers in the same direction from the array; and (iii) a verifiable reward for alignment: the named speaker must sit where the sound comes from, the confidence must match that check, and always answering "group" earns nothing. Every decision is online, with the lookahead as an explicit parameter. On 9,498 addressee-labelled utterances from three AMI sites and egocentric EasyCom, the direction prompt raises speaker accuracy from 54% to 74%; addressee accuracy moves by about two points, because telling one addressee from the group depends on the words more than on where people are. For co-located speakers on AVDIAR, visual active-speaker detection holds 95% where direction alone falls to 59%. The verifiable reward beats self-evaluation under GRPO, reward-ranked fine-tuning and DPO; shrinking the lookahead from 5 s to 0.5 s costs 4.5 DER points on AMI, and learning the registration online costs a dip in the first two minutes before the system overtakes vision alone. Abstract (英文): World action models (WAMs) typically adapt large pretrained video generators to jointly predict future observations and robot actions, incurring substantial training and deployment costs. However, it remains unclear which future representations a policy actually needs and how compactly they can be represented. Our probing study shows that complementary future representations can be compressed into a few tokens while retaining most of their action-decoding performance. Motivated by this finding, we introduce LeanWAM, a latent WAM that transfers video priors from a frozen video generator to a lightweight policy via two-stage training. In the first stage, a Future Extractor compresses observed transitions into 32 predictive tokens. These tokens condition the frozen generator and are jointly shaped by action, RGB, semantic, and geometric supervision, while a compact readout of the generator's hidden states supplies complementary world tokens. The second stage distills both representations into the policy, which learns to predict them from current observations while generating actions. With 1.4B parameters and no large-scale embodied pretraining, LeanWAM outperforms strong vision-language-action (VLA) and WAM baselines on LIBERO, LIBERO-Plus, and RoboTwin~2.0. It predicts an action chunk in 25.1\,ms on an A100 GPU, 12.3--41.5$\times$ faster than representative WAMs. These results demonstrate that compact predictive supervision can transfer video priors to robust and efficient control.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Harnesses for vision-language models (VLMs) have grown with the models' capabilities, notably for images far larger than those seen in training, where downscaling loses small details. Most of them wrap the VLM in a fixed procedure, such as a zoom tree, that decides in advance where it looks and how the evidence is combined. These procedures make up for the weaknesses of older models by keeping them on a task-specific path, but with frontier models that reliably plan and use tools, they can lower accuracy. We propose RVLM-X, a task-generalizable recursive VLM harness that runs unchanged across photographs, charts and maps. Following the recursive inference of Recursive Language Models, the VLM programmatically controls its own visual context in a code REPL that holds the full-resolution image and calls itself recursively on regions that it chooses. With a cost-efficient frontier model as the base of every method, RVLM-X is more accurate than a single call on all 7 public benchmarks and has the highest pooled accuracy. Because existing benchmarks mostly test whether a model finds one small detail, we also introduce MapGenBench, procedurally generated maps whose questions combine fine-detail perception with multi-step reasoning. There, RVLM-X answers 71.4% of held-out questions against 55.5% for the strongest baseline. Overall, our findings suggest that as VLMs plan and use tools more reliably, letting the model write its own visual search works better than fixing one in advance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): 3D foundation models (3DFMs) enable dense reconstruction in a feed-forward pass, yet their effective span is limited by cross-view attention. Local consistency has been expanded through geometric alignment of video chunks and recursive updates of an implicit memory, and large-scale scenes with pose graphs. However, both strategies are prone to drift, largely due to the lack of metric-scale observability from monocular geometry. We present Xtream,a hybrid streaming pipeline that maintains an explicit metric state through a factor graph over keyframes, while using a 3DFM as a geometric front end. Xtream incorporates metric information in two ways: through metric scale predictions from 3DFMs; or through inertial measurements if available. Across the large-scale benchmarks of VBR, Oxford Spires and KITTI, Xtream achieves state-of-the-art performance in 3DFM-based online reconstruction, reducing average absolute trajectory errors compared with published baselines and concurrent work. https://ernestolozano.github.io/xtream

Abstract (英文): As live video streams grow longer, repeatedly encoding and processing their full histories becomes increasingly costly. Answering questions as they arrive therefore calls for visual memory built in one pass before future questions are known. Existing methods mainly keep tokens that differ from earlier ones or match surrogate queries fixed in advance. Without modeling world dynamics, these criteria struggle to tell subtle surprising details from expected change. We introduce Dreamcatcher, a training-free framework and, to our knowledge, the first to use predictive signals from an independently pretrained, frozen video world model for online memory formation in streaming video question answering. Conditioned on recent history, the world model predicts incoming features using learned world priors. Its normalized prediction errors yield temporal and spatial surprisal signals that guide online segmentation and the retention of a frozen Video-LLM's native visual tokens, balancing surprising details with broad visual coverage under budgets that decay with segment age. At query time, an evidence gate admits a segment from a similarity-based shortlist only when the Video-LLM judges that it adds evidence for the question beyond the current view. We evaluate Dreamcatcher on three streaming and three offline long-video benchmarks. On OVO-Bench, Dreamcatcher achieves the highest overall accuracy among the compared training-free methods for each of four frozen Video-LLMs, reaching 78.9% with Qwen3.8-27B. With the three Qwen Video-LLMs, it also reduces average input tokens for answer generation by more than 80% relative to their base models at 1 fps.

</details>

Abstract (英文): While text-driven body motion generation has matured, pure-hand synthesis remains bottlenecked by data. Existing datasets often treat hands as rigid end-effectors, lack fine-grained captions, or are too limited in scale to support robust generalization. We introduce RoMo Hands, an in-the-wild, text-paired dataset of ~542K sequences (884 hours, 95.5M frames at 30 fps) which is an order of magnitude larger than prior work. To ensure high-quality supervision, a stringent dexterity filter discards low-articulation sequences, focusing the distribution on complex bimanual movement. Each clip is paired with five captions at increasing granularities from global semantic tags to atomic finger states, organized under a hierarchical taxonomy. To bridge the gap between body and hand architectures, we propose a novel 393-D bimanual representation that adapts redundant body-motion paradigms to the high-DoF kinematics of hands. Systematic benchmarking of diffusion, masked-token, and autoregressive generators reveals distinct trade-offs under these hand-specific adaptations, including a sharp divergence between semantic alignment and kinematic plausibility. Our dataset, representation, and benchmark establish a rigorous foundation for the next generation of dexterous motion synthesis. Project page: https://romodataset.github.io/romo-hands/.

Abstract (英文): Converting pretrained softmax Transformers to efficient attention often causes substantial performance degradation, while recovery typically requires costly fine-tuning. We propose ET (Efficient Transfer), a framework for high-fidelity attention conversion with minimal optimization cost. ET combines exact local and shared-key softmax attention with a mean-anchored, scan-free global linear correction and adaptive gating, achieving linear complexity in sequence length. To preserve pretrained knowledge, ET freezes pretrained weights and calibrates only newly introduced parameters through alternating least squares on a small set of unlabeled samples, without gradient updates. On ImageNet-1K, ET achieves 79.64% and 81.55% top-1 accuracy on DeiT-S and DeiT-B, respectively, within 0.25 percentage points of their softmax teachers. At $768\times768$, ET outperforms T$^5$ by 3.59 percentage points at comparable inference FLOPs, without gradient-based fine-tuning. On DiT-XL/2, ET achieves an FID-50K of 2.59 with only 250 LoRA updates. On Stable Diffusion 3.5 Medium, ET matches the teacher's GenEval score at 1K resolution with just 0.73 A100 GPU-hours of gradient-free conversion and a 1.39$\times$ inference speedup.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Adversarial perturbation defenses protect images from unauthorized edits by adding imperceptible noise. Yet none of these methods have been evaluated against nudification, despite AI-generated non-consensual intimate imagery (AIG-NCII) being among the most documented harms of generative AI. Moreover, nudification edits are readily carried out through inpainting, the primary setting these protection methods are designed to defend against. We argue this is a problem-formulation failure since prior threat models do not correspond to nudification edits (also called attacks). The misalignment of existing approaches is structural, spanning mask shape, training and testing prompt distributions, and success criterion which causes existing evaluations to systematically overestimate protection effectiveness. We reformulate image protection against unauthorized editing around this documented harm. We introduce BODYBENCH, a benchmark of 648 fully synthetic clothed adult subjects, each paired with 11 mask variants spanning body, face, and context regions. We propose three harm-aligned metrics: sexualization, naturalness, and recognizability that jointly determine whether an inpainted output constitutes a successful nudification attack. Auditing four recent protection methods (PhotoGuard, DiffusionGuard, AdvPaint, DiffVax) on BODYBENCH, we find that protection succeeds in only one configuration of one method, and only under exact mask alignment between defender and attacker---a condition no real defender can guarantee and one that we show is easily always bypassed. We also show that standard evaluation metrics such as peak signal-to-noise ratio (PSNR) are essentially uncorrelated with nudification protection. BODYBENCH makes this misalignment measurable and provides a foundation for defenses grounded in documented harm.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Sequential concept acquisition enables interpretable diagnosis from a compact set of human-understandable findings. Such explanations are useful only if clinicians can trust them, yet most existing methods treat predicted concepts as correct, so a correct diagnosis can rest on incorrectly predicted evidence. We propose Reliable Concept Acquisition with Pruning (ReCAP), which reasons over what the concept predictor reports, including errors and abstentions, and replaces conditional information gain with a label-discriminating objective that separates cases requiring different diagnoses. We prove this objective adaptively monotone and adaptively submodular, yielding a (1-1/e) guarantee for greedy fixed-budget acquisition that degrades boundedly under learned estimates, and combine it with abstention-aware acquisition and diagnosis-preserving pruning to keep explanations short and reliable. Across five medical imaging datasets, ReCAP reduces the share of correct diagnoses supported by at least one incorrectly predicted concept from 27.1% to 9.6% relative to the strongest baseline, while using fewer than half as many concepts and achieving higher diagnostic accuracy.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The Navier-Stokes (NS) inpainting model of Bertalmío, Bertozzi and Sapiro is the only transport-type paradigm among PDE-based methods, yet it has never received a systematic higher-order extension. We show that the obvious fourth-order correction is ill-posed, and we construct the corrected, well-posed model. In the vorticity-streamfunction formulation we add a gated fourth-order term whose sign is decisive: the backward form is a Perona-Malik-type anti-diffusion, Hadamard ill-posed and consistently harmful in experiments, whereas the forward form is uniformly parabolic, energy-stable and consistently beneficial. An order-dissipation principle, stating that odd orders are dispersive and even orders dissipative, singles out the fourth order as the lowest admissible one. Large missing blocks pose a separate problem, because the correction gate stays silent there; we answer it with stripe decomposition, a Telea base fill followed by interleaved slit-wise NS passes, which converts block damage into the structural-slit regime where the model excels. The device is base-agnostic, and its gains add to those of the forward correction. On three datasets, namely BSDS500, DIV2K and a classical Chinese painting benchmark, and on three damage types, namely cracks, blocks and text occlusion, the training-free model beats every classical baseline on every sample of structure-dominated damage, gains +1.02 dB PSNR over Telea on the full 42-image painting test set with a 42/42 per-sample win rate, matches the strongest recent PDE solvers within 0.4 dB while attaining the best perceptual quality of the entire PDE family, and runs at 4-16 s per image on a consumer GPU.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent time-series foundation models (TSFMs) forecast a distribution over futures, and multimodal time-series (TS) forecasting further exploits other modalities to improve forecasting accuracy. However, existing works have two limitations: 1) TSFMs reduce the predictive distribution to its median or mean, flattening diverse futures into a calm line, and 2) multimodal TS forecasting models incorporate text into the forecaster through TS-language alignment, which is substantially weaker than vision-language alignment. To address these limitations, we propose the VIsion-language SelectOR (VISOR), a multimodal TS forecasting framework that selects one plausible future instead of averaging across candidates, leveraging the alignment capabilities of vision-language models (VLMs) to select the most suitable candidate based on textual information. VISOR consists of two components: 1) a text-free stochastic predictor that samples diverse candidate futures, and 2) a VLM selector that reads the candidates as a single chart image and selects one based on the text. To avoid overly calm forecasting paths, we train the selector with a shape-aware target that evaluates each candidate based on both point-wise error and path shape. Across three benchmarks covering ten datasets, VISOR achieves the best average ranking in both point and volatility errors compared with TSFMs and multimodal TS forecasting models. Code: https://github.com/seunghan96/VISOR.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Interactive video world models generate navigable worlds frame by frame, but a persistent world requires previously observed scenes to remain recoverable as users move away and return. Existing memory evaluations, however, often focus on seen-view revisits and full rollouts, leaving cross-view scene recovery underexplored while entangling memory with trajectory-following errors and accumulated generation degradation. We introduce SceneMemBench, a benchmark for scene memory in interactive video world models. It evaluates two complementary dimensions: seen-view recall versus novel-view recovery, and controlled diagnosis versus end-to-end interaction. We introduce Continued Rollout from a shared ground-truth observation history, alongside Full Rollout over complete model-generated interactions. SceneMemBench contains 600 controlled trajectories across 33 Unreal Engine scenes and 60 Full Rollout cases, covering eight representative open- and closed-source models. We evaluate scene recovery along subject consistency, appearance fidelity, and geometric consistency. Results show that novel-view recovery is generally more challenging than seen-view recall, while model profiles under controlled histories can differ markedly from those observed in full interaction. Together, these findings reveal complementary failure modes obscured by seen-view revisits or end-to-end rollouts alone.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reconstructing the 3D poses of hands and objects from a single image, together with the contact between them, is fundamental to understanding manipulation. To be practical, the reconstruction must generalize to unseen objects, and the contact must be localized precisely. Yet joint feed-forward estimators often rely on predefined object sets, while contact is typically predicted from image features alone, without the explicit geometry needed to localize it. To address both limitations, we present HORCRUX, a unified feed-forward transformer with coarse-to-fine contact estimation. For unseen objects, a frozen foundation model encodes the object from a given or image-reconstructed mesh rather than from object-specific knowledge. A shared transformer then reasons jointly over the object and one or two hands to keep them aligned. For precise contact, coarse per-joint predictions from latent features are resolved over finer hand regions using the reconstructed geometry. Across seven benchmarks, HORCRUX achieves state-of-the-art object and hand pose accuracy among joint estimators and outperforms a dedicated contact estimator in fine-grained contact. These results suggest that contact is best estimated jointly with reconstruction, whose geometry directly localizes it.

</details>

Abstract (英文): World action models (WAMs) adapt pretrained video generators for robot control by jointly predicting future camera frames and actions, transferring physical priors learned from video into manipulation policies. However, generating future frames before every action makes test-time inference too slow for closed-loop control, while most existing policies remain restricted to short observation histories. Building on Fast-WAM, which showed that video prediction shapes policy representations during training so that future frame synthesis can be skipped at deployment, we present G0.7, a generalist world action model scaled along two axes: extending the context window from a single observation to up to five minutes of history, and scaling pretraining to a large-scale corpus of robot teleoperation and human egocentric (ego) video. Across public simulation suites and real-robot deployments, direct action decoding (Fast mode) matches imagine-then-act (IDM) inference from the same checkpoint while running in 60 ms per 16-step action chunk on a single RTX 5090 (7.6x faster than IDM) and supporting pause-free real-time chunking. Performance scales consistently with pretraining data, with human ego video driving the largest gains in out-of-distribution generalization: G0.7 achieves a 70.8 mean score across five simulation benchmark suites, 48% and 54% zero-shot pick-and-place success on Galaxea R1 Pro and R1 Lite, 96.7% in-distribution and 93.3% out-of-distribution success on video-conditioned in-context learning tasks, and 86.9% mean task progress across seven real-world post-training tasks including multi-minute box folding, drink preparation, and product bagging.

Abstract (英文): Where a person looks indicates their focus of attention, and how their gaze shifts between targets reveals how that attention responds to a changing scene. Existing gaze target estimation methods largely operate frame by frame and do not model these shifts. Gaze shifts are often driven by dynamic changes in the scene, which is the kind of information world models are designed to encode. This relationship is subtle, however, since an event far across the frame may produce only a slight movement of the person's head or eyes. We therefore introduce the Gaze World Model (GWM), which co-trains latent scene prediction with person-conditioned gaze supervision on a frozen V-JEPA 2.1 encoder; in our ablations, the latent prediction objective improves both shift detection and gaze localization. Given a video clip and the person's head box, GWM estimates gaze locations and detects gaze shifts in the observed clip, and forecasts future gaze and shifts from predicted scene features. To train and evaluate these tasks, we augment VideoAttentionTarget and ChildPlay with event-level gaze-shift annotations. GWM achieves state-of-the-art shift detection on these benchmarks, about 10 points higher in four-class AP than the strongest baseline, while remaining comparable to the strongest image-based models in gaze target estimation, and outperforms the compared baselines on future gaze localization and shift forecasting.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Joint-embedding predictive architectures learn by predicting features of masked regions from visible context. In chest computed tomography, the findings that decide a diagnosis, such as a small nodule, are often not predictable from the surrounding anatomy. A hidden finding is then best predicted toward normal anatomy, and the prediction loss stops rewarding the encoder for representing it. We call this the context shortcut. Conditioning the predictor on the radiology report repairs the prediction, but in our setup the encoder gains little over image-only pretraining: the report supplies what the encoder never had to learn. JECT therefore gives the report two roles: a condition for the predictor and, through masked section-text prediction, a target for the encoder, which must predict the report's anatomical sections from the unmasked scan. A hierarchical three-dimensional encoder that predicts at every stage keeps this clinical content at every scale. In ablations, the text target improves classification and localization, while conditioning adds little to the encoder. Trained from scratch on 173k volumes, the recipe reaches the level of current computed tomography foundation models in classification, retrieval and nodule malignancy, and of task-specific models in lung-cancer risk prediction after fine-tuning. Conditioning still matters: its predictor can be reused, with no extra training, to show where a report sentence acts, changing most at the finding the sentence describes. Code and pretrained weights are available.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Zero-shot skeleton-based action recognition is evaluated almost exclusively on flat leaf labels, which leaves open whether a representation also grounds the broader concepts that contain an action in skeleton motion. We find that strong leaf alignment does not carry over to them: when ancestor concepts are scored directly under independent decoding, eight flat methods with 57.86-73.29% mean leaf Top-1 reach only 6.15-31.21% mean full-path accuracy across four NTU RGB+D splits. We propose the Hyperbolic Hierarchical Semantic Prototype ($H^2SP$) framework, which represents every node of an action taxonomy in the Lorentz model of hyperbolic space. First, instead of learning a free vector per node, $H^2SP$ synthesizes hierarchy-aware prototypes by recursive parent-child geodesic interpolation under a single tree-wide gate. Second, tangent-space semantic and distributional alignment on seen classes anchor the leaf prototypes in skeleton motion. Third, a soft entailment-cone regularizer encourages directional parent-child inclusion, propagating this motion grounding to ancestor concepts. We evaluate hierarchical concept prediction under independent and cascade decoding, and transfer to an alternative taxonomy without retraining. Across the four splits, $H^2SP$ attains the best mean independent and cascade full-path accuracy among the evaluated methods, at a comparable leaf Top-1 of 72.40%. Transferred to the alternative taxonomy, it outperforms both flat baselines and the same-tree Euclidean control across every split and decoding mode, indicating that its concept-level correspondence extends beyond the training taxonomy.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Old and new document embeddings coexist during incremental gallery re-encoding. RELAY trains a new query encoder with a learned projection against the original gallery, then freezes both to train the new document encoder. The same query vector scores both document versions throughout a single re-encoding pass. After backfill, optional query refinement drops the projection and reuses the stored document vectors. Across three text-to-image retrieval upgrades on COCO and Flickr30K, RELAY improves Recall@1 on untouched galleries by 8.66-10.23 percentage points over the original systems. For direct re-encoding, RELAY ranks first in both Qwen-to-Qwen upgrades and second in CLIP-to-Qwen by area under the Recall@1 curve, under both native scoring and matched calibration. With embeddings held fixed, calibrating scores separately for each document version can reduce drops in Recall@1 during backfill and change which method achieves the highest backfill AUC. After query refinement, final Recall@1 is within 0.97 percentage points of conventional joint training in all three upgrades.

Abstract (英文): Video understanding depends not only on how models reason about visual evidence, but also on which evidence they observe. Fixed observation policies determine this evidence in advance; agentic harnesses allow models to seek further observations as their understanding develops. This shift creates an opportunity to realize capabilities that fixed inputs leave untapped, yet the ability to request observations does not ensure their effective use. We investigate when and why video harnesses improve understanding, examining how task demands and models' capacity for interaction jointly shape their benefits. Using Video-Relook as an experimental framework, we let models adapt their temporal and spatial observations from a common default configuration, and analyze performance and interaction trajectories across models and tasks. Adaptive observation yields substantial gains on fine-grained action understanding, with benefits also extending to Video-MME-v2. Even under the same observation interface, gains differ substantially across models; interaction traces reveal corresponding differences in evidence seeking and the reliability of answer revision. The benefits also vary across tasks for the same model, ranging from substantial improvements to limited gains and regressions. Together, these findings support an account in which harness effectiveness depends on the alignment between opportunities for informative observation and the model's ability to turn interaction into reliable revisions. They motivate video harnesses that coordinate perception and reasoning, matching observational autonomy to task demands and model capabilities.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Training capable omni video agents requires more than diverse question-answer pairs: agents must learn to reliably find, connect, and correct task-relevant evidence in omni-setting. However, we argue that existing data pipelines provide limited control over task-relevant evidence and weak supervision for these capabilities. In this work, we present Open OmniCoach, a new framework for evidence-seeking omni video agents. It contains a high-quality dataset, OmniCoach-240K, and a new agentic reinforcement learning (RL) method, Step-Level Evidence-Guided Policy Optimization (SEPO). For the former, OmniCoach-240K comprises 248.2K interaction trajectories, including 230.7K multi-span trajectories and 17.4K reflection trajectories. We design a three-stage data pipeline by leveraging both fine-grained omni-evidence and the agent's own errors. Meanwhile, compared with other RL baselines, SEPO provides turn-level credit based on improvements in evidence coverage and temporal localization. Applying both trajectory data and the SEPO method, we obtain Open OmniAgent models. The resulting models outperform the baselines across multiple video and audio-visual understanding tasks by significant margins, demonstrating the effectiveness of the Open OmniCoach framework. In particular, Open OmniAgent-30B improves average accuracy over Qwen3-Omni by 6.3 percentage points across nine benchmarks, with gains of 12.2 and 13.3 points on LVOmniBench and LVBench, respectively.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Knowledge-intensive video generation remains challenging because prompts often omit the facts, appearances, and temporal relationships required for accurate generation. While search-based workflows can retrieve missing evidence, manually designed strategies limit adaptive planning, and conventional training suffers from costly video-level feedback and coarse trajectory-level credit assignment. In this paper, we present Loupe, a video generation agent that retrieves and verifies textual and visual evidence to construct evidence-grounded temporal video prompts with reference images. We further introduce Rubric-Based Credit (RBC), which leverages prompt-specific factual, visual, and temporal requirements to evaluate intermediate search and planning decisions before video rendering. Building on RBC, RBC-SFT reweights assistant-token learning by step quality, while RBC-GRPO incorporates step-level credits alongside a final planning reward, without rendering videos during RL. To address the lack of dedicated search-oriented evaluation, we construct Search-VBench, which contains 150 held-out prompts across seven categories. Experiments show that Loupe improves all four evaluation dimensions across three video generators, raising the overall score of MiniMax-H3 from 32.80 to 70.67 and outperforming an untrained search workflow. Ablation experiments show that full RBC training outperforms standard SFT followed by trajectory-level GRPO by 5.27 points. Human evaluations and results on Sci-VBench and KIVI-Bench further demonstrate improved generation quality, scientific grounding, factual precision, and helpfulness. Our project page is available at https://zengkaiya.github.io/Loupe/.
